## Where to start
- IS-J01-20241001-EST-ENTRY — the only file for this month and the earliest dated entry in Part 01; it sets out the repeated phrases and the range of locations.

## The author's own entries
The entry is the author's own summary note. It contains no Questions & Comments section and no verbatim transcript; the quoted phrases are given from memory with an estimated count.


===== RECAP: summaries/IS_SUM_J01_2025-02.md · ID: IS-SUM-J01-2025-02 =====

# Journal Part 01 — February 2025

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 2 entries and 0 recordings, February 2025 (one entry with an estimated date, one dated 27 February) · about 630 words · source: `journal/part-01/`, files dated 2025-02.

## What this month contains
Two manual-capture entries with no audio. The first, IS-J01-20250201-EST-ENTRY, is undated in the source ("Feb 2025, Estimated") and summarises about a month of night and early-morning statements at the building where the author was living. The second, IS-J01-20250227-ENTRY, records an afternoon near a coffee shop in Denver and is the longer of the two, mostly taken up by the author's Questions & Comments. Both entries mark the quoted lines as paraphrasing.

## Recurring statements
- **Euthanization.** The first entry says that almost every night for a month statements urged the author to go outside and accept euthanization; it also notes the author's view that a sound weapon may have been in use for about two weeks before he began taking notes (IS-J01-20250201-EST-ENTRY).
- **Claimed responsibility for an attack.** The 27 February entry records a voice the author describes as a Middle Eastern male claiming responsibility for a terrorist attack on the United States and claiming membership of a foreign royal family (IS-J01-20250227-ENTRY).
- **Repeating statements on request.** The same entry says the author was asked to repeat the statement and did so, prefacing it with his own name to separate himself from it (IS-J01-20250227-ENTRY).
- **Control of the communication method.** Statements in the entry suggest a federal intelligence agency also sought to claim responsibility for the method, paraphrased as "We absolutely need control of this method of communication" (IS-J01-20250227-ENTRY).
- **Grievance.** A paraphrased line frames the statements as a response to something the United States did (IS-J01-20250227-ENTRY).

## Where to start
- IS-J01-20250227-ENTRY — the main entry of the month, with the attack claim and the author's longest commentary so far.
- IS-J01-20250201-EST-ENTRY — short, and the first entry in Part 01 to record statements referencing euthanization.

## The author's own entries
In IS-J01-20250227-ENTRY the author's Questions & Comments question the speaker's claimed identity, note that a voice may be disguised, state that he knows of no attack publicly connected to the date and makes no claim that one occurred, and ask whether the grievance has a referent. He cites outside reporting from months later on military use of a commercial cloud platform, while stating explicitly that it is dated after the entry and that he claims no connection.


===== RECAP: summaries/IS_SUM_J01_2025-03.md · ID: IS-SUM-J01-2025-03 =====

# Journal Part 01 — March 2025

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 10 entries and 0 recordings, 1–20 March 2025 (two entries with estimated dates) · about 5,300 words · source: `journal/part-01/`, files dated 2025-03.

## What this month contains
All ten files are manual-capture entries; there are no audio recordings. Most were written at a Denver apartment where the author was a temporary guest. The densest, 4–8 March, are timestamped logs listing statements line by line, each tagged with the speaker's apparent gender and whether it was "received telepathically" or "partially audible". Most entries open with a note on what the author calls possible use of a sound weapon: ringing ears, headache, nausea, chest vibration. On 9 March he writes that he tried to stop keeping the journal (IS-J01-20250309-ENTRY); the estimated 20 March entry describes the resulting gap (IS-J01-20250320-EST-ENTRY).

## Recurring statements
- **Harm to family members.** An estimated-date entry records statements, which the author says began around August 2024, claiming that his former wife and young daughter had been killed (IS-J01-20250301-EST-ENTRY). The author writes that he has been unable to reach them (IS-J01-20250304-ENTRY).
- **Euthanization and death threats.** Statements referencing euthanization, "smothering" people with information, and someone being told to "take the shot" recur across the week (IS-J01-20250304-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250305-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250308-ENTRY).
- **Agencies and groups.** Statements name federal law-enforcement and intelligence agencies, a "root work community", and several foreign countries, and repeatedly include shouted commands such as "hands up" that the author says sound like they come from outside (IS-J01-20250304-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250305-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250307-ENTRY).
- **Note-taking.** Many statements react to the author writing notes, telling him to stop or commenting on them, and he writes that someone seems to read along as he types (IS-J01-20250304-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250309-2-ENTRY).
- **Danger to the building.** Statements on 8 March say the building's ventilation contains poison gas; the author records submitting an anonymous tip (IS-J01-20250308-ENTRY).
- **Technology versus spirituality.** Statements debate whether the phenomenon is "spirituality" or technology (IS-J01-20250303-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250304-ENTRY).

## Where to start
- IS-J01-20250304-ENTRY — the longest entry of the month; a full day of timestamped lines covering most recurring themes.
- IS-J01-20250301-EST-ENTRY — the earliest account of the statements about family members, with background to August 2024.
- IS-J01-20250308-ENTRY — the ventilation statements and the author's decision to submit a tip.
- IS-J01-20250320-EST-ENTRY — explains the gap in entries that follows.

## The author's own entries
The author's notes run inline with the logged statements; there are no separate Questions & Comments sections. He records physical effects and visual impressions of the speakers, asks how to tell remembered music from music he hears from outside, whether his devices are compromised, and whether the speakers can tell his private thoughts from intended replies. He also records his job search and his uncertainty about sharing the journal.


===== RECAP: summaries/IS_SUM_J01_2025-04.md · ID: IS-SUM-J01-2025-04 =====

# Journal Part 01 — April 2025

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 6 entries and 0 recordings, 7–30 April 2025 (one entry with an estimated date; two note that the exact day is uncertain) · about 1,350 words · source: `journal/part-01/`, files dated 2025-04.

## What this month contains
Six short manual-capture entries, written mainly at the Denver apartment building where the author was staying, with one written while walking on a city street. The author had said in March that he was taking a break from the journal, and these entries are sparser: brief notes rather than timestamped logs. The longest, 22 and 29 April, are bulleted summaries of statements over an evening and a night, followed by the author's reflections.

## Recurring statements
- **Ongoing effects.** The author notes that what he calls daily tormenting communication and possible use of a sound weapon continue (IS-J01-20250407-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250413-ENTRY).
- **Nanotechnology.** A statement says nanotechnology has been dispersed into communities by someone who worked on it (IS-J01-20250413-ENTRY).
- **Foreign policy and agencies.** The author summarises statements calling for the United States to withdraw from a foreign country, referencing a federal law-enforcement agency, a "root work community", a technology company's subsidiary and a virtual-reality product, and a communications regulator (IS-J01-20250422-ENTRY).
- **Public service announcements.** Statements thank the author for "public service announcements" and ask him to continue them and his journal (IS-J01-20250422-ENTRY).
- **The apartment above.** Statements warn the author to stay away from the apartment upstairs, with one contradicting the others; the author links them to two people he calls "telepathic characters D and E" (IS-J01-20250425-EST-ENTRY).
- **A suggested killing.** Verbal and visual suggestions describe a person being euthanized and a body being concealed in a neighbouring apartment; the author states that these may be suggestions rather than events and could falsely implicate his neighbours (IS-J01-20250429-ENTRY).
- **His own voice outside.** The author writes that he may have heard his own thoughts playing from a passing vehicle (IS-J01-20250430-ENTRY).

## Where to start
- IS-J01-20250422-ENTRY — the fullest list of the month's statements and the author's account of requests for protection.
- IS-J01-20250429-ENTRY — the longest entry, with the author's "in retrospect" reflections on what the suggestions might or might not mean.
- IS-J01-20250413-ENTRY — the first mention of nanotechnology in Part 01.

## The author's own entries
The author's notes summarise rather than transcribe. He asks whether other residents hear the same communications, records that he has repeatedly asked for protection for himself and his family without any response, describes his emotional state and physical health, and in IS-J01-20250429-ENTRY explicitly considers that the suggestions may describe nothing that happened, or something that happened elsewhere. He ends one entry asking for an in-person demonstration (IS-J01-20250430-ENTRY).


===== RECAP: summaries/IS_SUM_J01_2025-05.md · ID: IS-SUM-J01-2025-05 =====

# Journal Part 01 — May 2025

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 14 entries and 0 recordings, 1–29 May 2025 · about 11,300 words · source: `journal/part-01/`, files dated 2025-05.

## What this month contains
All fourteen files are manual-capture entries; there are no audio recordings. In mid-May the author records losing his guest stay at the apartment building (IS-J01-20250515-ENTRY) and moving to a homeless shelter; from then on he writes from the shelter, coffee shops and a homeless resource center. On 3 May he writes that he is reluctantly resuming the journal because of continued death threats (IS-J01-20250503-ENTRY). By 27–29 May entries are long runs of lines typed as heard, tagged "Male #?" or "Female #?", and the statements increasingly react to his note-taking.

## Recurring statements
- **Euthanization.** Requests that the author "accept" euthanization, and statements debating whether to carry it out, run through the month (IS-J01-20250515-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250528-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250529-ENTRY). Some statements threaten violence with a weapon (IS-J01-20250528-ENTRY).
- **A city office.** Over several days statements suggest the author is communicating with staff of a city government office, including a statement opposing covert assisted death that the author transcribes (IS-J01-20250515-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250517-ENTRY). The author notes this may be impersonation.
- **The shelter.** Statements describe staff and unidentified visitors debating residents' welfare and equipment in the building (IS-J01-20250520-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250525-ENTRY).
- **Foreign attack claims.** Statements again claim a terrorist attack and name a foreign country, framed as retaliation (IS-J01-20250525-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250529-ENTRY).
- **Reaction to transcripts.** Many lines say the author is "capturing actual transcripts" and urge him to stop, e.g. "It appears that this person is capturing actual transcripts" (IS-J01-20250527-ENTRY; also IS-J01-20250528-ENTRY).
- **Family members.** A phone call the author says he recorded reports his former wife and daughter are safe (IS-J01-20250512-ENTRY); later statements again describe harm to them (IS-J01-20250517-ENTRY).
- **Physical effects and a corporation.** The author records a loud high-pitched sound, stomach upset and chest symptoms, with statements attributing them to named organizations (IS-J01-20250512-2-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250528-ENTRY).

## Where to start
- IS-J01-20250529-ENTRY — the longest entry so far; two sessions covering most themes, including statements promising extraction or witness protection.
- IS-J01-20250517-ENTRY — the city-office statements and the author's transcribed statement opposing covert assisted death.
- IS-J01-20250519-ENTRY — the move to the shelter and the author's longest Questions & Comments.

## The author's own entries
The author's Questions & Comments sections grow this month. He records housing loss, hunger and job search, counts how often themes have recurred since February, and repeatedly asks why no one contacts him by phone or email. In IS-J01-20250519-ENTRY he records his own statements refusing euthanization, including one referencing a ritual suicide as a form of protest, and states in the same entry that he is not suicidal. He also notes alternative ordinary explanations where he has them, such as a sinus infection (IS-J01-20250528-ENTRY).


===== RECAP: summaries/IS_SUM_J01_2025-06.md · ID: IS-SUM-J01-2025-06 =====

# Journal Part 01 — June 2025

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 5 entries and 0 recordings, 8–11 June 2025 · about 7,500 words · source: `journal/part-01/`, files dated 2025-06.

## What this month contains
Five manual-capture entries written over four consecutive days, 8–11 June; there are no dated files for the rest of the month. The author is staying at a homeless shelter in Denver and writes from places of worship, a homeless resource center and a public library. Two entries add a "Methodology" line (manual capture on phone or laptop). Several sections are marked "In progress" with placeholders, and some Questions & Comments sections are empty. IS-J01-20250611-2-ENTRY, at about 4,300 words, is the longest single entry in Part 01 before the August recordings; it includes a later note dated July 2025.

## Recurring statements
- **Violence in visual suggestions.** The author describes visual suggestions of violent acts against a man and a child, which he says he partly felt physically; for the child, he records that further "illumination" showed a calm, uninjured child (IS-J01-20250608-ENTRY).
- **Retraction of the attack claim.** On 9 June a voice previously described as representing a foreign royal family is recorded retracting its February claim of a terrorist attack and apologising (IS-J01-20250609-ENTRY; compare IS-J01-20250608-ENTRY).
- **Spirituality and the military.** Statements at a church call it a heresy and say telepathic communication is "for the military only" (IS-J01-20250608-ENTRY).
- **Euthanization and threats.** Requests that the author accept euthanization and statements threatening him and his family recur, alongside other voices objecting (IS-J01-20250610-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250611-2-ENTRY).
- **A university and students.** A voice says it is a student required by an instructor to take part; the author calls this a pattern (IS-J01-20250609-ENTRY).
- **Mind control and technology.** Statements mention mind control, a foreign country, extraterrestrial technology, a brain-computer interface, a technology company and a federal drug-enforcement agency's "system" (IS-J01-20250610-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250611-2-ENTRY).
- **A change of speakers.** Partway through the library session the author records the abusive voices stopping and a different group, without "voice filters", saying they are not allowed to speak to him (IS-J01-20250611-2-ENTRY).

## Where to start
- IS-J01-20250609-ENTRY — the retraction of the attack claim and the university statements.
- IS-J01-20250611-2-ENTRY — the longest entry; the change of speakers and the author's longest Questions & Comments.
- IS-J01-20250608-ENTRY — the visual suggestions and the church statements that precede the retraction.

## The author's own entries
The author records his own replies, marked "Myself". In Questions & Comments he asks whether the original attack claim was false and why, why speakers seem surprised he can hear them, and whether his laptop camera or a location he typed was used to find him. He compares the shelter with the apartment and records sharing his contact details without reply.


===== RECAP: summaries/IS_SUM_J01_2025-07.md · ID: IS-SUM-J01-2025-07 =====

# Journal Part 01 — July 2025

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 13 entries and 52 recordings, 2–28 July 2025 · about 105,600 words · source: `journal/part-01/`, files dated 2025-07.

## What this month contains
Three typed entries from 2 July were written at a shelter, on a bus and at a day center (IS-J01-20250702-ENTRY). On 10 July the author begins repeating statements aloud into a phone recorder and transcribing the audio with an AI tool. All 52 recordings fall between 10 and 17 July, made at an intersection where he holds a donation sign, on walks and scooter rides around Denver, and outside shelters. On 11 July he loses his shelter bed for arriving after curfew. That night's recordings are angry, and in some of them the author demands euthanization from unseen parties. His own notes say these were said in anger and that he does not accept euthanization (IS-J01-20250711-ENTRY, IS-J01-20250711-R13). Entries for 12–16 July are stubs listing recordings; the month closes with a typed entry (IS-J01-20250727-ENTRY) and a summary note (IS-J01-20250728-ENTRY).

## Recurring statements
- **Being recorded.** Statements react to the recordings: the transcripts are "word for word" and "incriminating", and he is told to stop (IS-J01-20250710-R02, IS-J01-20250711-R02).
- **The intersection.** Passing drivers are told "Do not support this person." A "battle for the intersection" is named, and statements and the author say that repeating the statements cuts this by about half (IS-J01-20250710-R03, IS-J01-20250711-R06, IS-J01-20250713-R04).
- **Euthanization and execution.** Statements urge the author to accept euthanization, describe it as ethical or a service, or threaten to execute him. Other voices object (IS-J01-20250711-R01, IS-J01-20250712-R02, IS-J01-20250715-R01).
- **"Speakerphone."** This is the author's name for statements he hears as audible sound coming from street noise. Statements link it to cell towers and satellites, and he asks whether other people hear it too (IS-J01-20250710-R06, IS-J01-20250712-R03, IS-J01-20250717-R03).
- **Breaching.** Statements describe things the author saw or felt, and he records these as possible evidence that others share his senses. He asks what this means for passwords and finances (IS-J01-20250715-R01, IS-J01-20250717-R06).
- **Automation and rotation.** Statements and the author raise the idea of AI voice agents. "Let's move on to the next person" is described as marking rotating groups (IS-J01-20250712-R01, IS-J01-20250715-R02).
- **Named organizations.** A federal security agency, a technology company, a drug-enforcement agency and foreign governments are named often. The author notes that names are sometimes appended to supportive statements (IS-J01-20250712-R03, IS-J01-20250713-R01).
- **A nuclear-strike statement.** On 17 July a statement claims a coming nuclear strike. The author treats it as character defamation and lists the homes he has lost (IS-J01-20250717-R05, IS-J01-20250717-R06).

## Where to start
- IS-J01-20250710-R07: the first recording day's long author note and its Questions & Comments on the intersection.
- IS-J01-20250711-ENTRY: the day the bed is lost, with the author's warning about the recordings that follow.
- IS-J01-20250713-R01: the author's framework, which looks for "patterns of behavior" rather than identities and frames the statements in terms of menacing and consent law.
- IS-J01-20250712-R03: how speakerphone works in his account, appended names, and breaching.
- IS-J01-20250717-R06: the nuclear-strike statement and the list of lost homes.
- IS-J01-20250728-ENTRY: the author's own summary of the month.

## The author's own entries
The author's spoken "administrative notes" make up much of the recordings. He timestamps and locates himself, offers theories about the technology and asks whether bystanders hear it. He proposes a high-court test case and consent-based rules for telepathic communication, and asks for help for his daughter and ex-wife. In IS-J01-20250713-R06 he recounts a suicide attempt from before the statements began, which he stopped by seeking hospital care. Questions & Comments appear in IS-J01-20250710-R07, IS-J01-20250711-R17 and IS-J01-20250727-ENTRY. In the 28 July note he says that without financial support he cannot continue editing the document.


===== RECAP: summaries/IS_SUM_J01_2025-08.md · ID: IS-SUM-J01-2025-08 =====

# Journal Part 01 — August 2025

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 8 entries and 30 recordings, 4–22 August 2025 · about 52,000 words · source: `journal/part-01/`, files dated 2025-08. Part 01 ends on 22 August.

## What this month contains
This is the first month in Part 01 with audio. Each entry is a short day page with a "Methodology" and "Scene" note and a list of that day's recordings. The author describes the method: he repeats aloud what he hears while recording on his phone, and an AI service transcribes the audio (IS-J01-20250804-ENTRY). Recordings alternate between a "repeating process" (statements spoken back as heard, often followed by "that's exactly what I said, word for word"), a "listening process" (silent gaps followed by the author's summary), and long "administrative notes" in which the author speaks for himself. Much of the text, especially from 12 August on, is administrative notes rather than repeated statements; most recordings are marked as paraphrasing.

Recording took place on walks across Denver, around a large homeless shelter where the author was staying, and at a street intersection where he stood with a cardboard sign, which the entries call the "battle for the intersection" (IS-J01-20250812-ENTRY onward). Activity is concentrated on 12 August (6 recordings) and 22 August (10 recordings).

## Recurring statements
- **"Do not support this person."** At the intersection, the recorded statements are mainly this line, said to be directed at passing drivers, sometimes adding "standing to your left" (IS-J01-20250812-R06, IS-J01-20250818-R02, IS-J01-20250822-R05).
- **A "euthanization list".** Statements say drivers who react should be added to a list; the author records four such suggestions on 22 August (IS-J01-20250822-R06, IS-J01-20250822-R09, IS-J01-20250822-R10).
- **Euthanization offered at the shelter.** The author describes statements offering euthanization at night, including a suggested service asking residents to raise their hands, and statements asking others to harm him (IS-J01-20250821-R01, IS-J01-20250822-R10).
- **Reactions to being recorded.** Statements comment that he is recording, that the transcripts are accurate, and ask him to stop (IS-J01-20250804-R01, IS-J01-20250820-R01, IS-J01-20250821-R03).
- **Agencies, companies and attack claims.** Statements name a national intelligence agency, a technology company said to make the "Citizen Tech" platform, a counter-terrorism presence and renewed claims of a terrorist attack; others deny or contradict these (IS-J01-20250812-R05, IS-J01-20250819-R01, IS-J01-20250821-R03). The first recording opens with "This is weapons development." (IS-J01-20250805-R01).
- **Promised help.** Statements suggest witness protection, a raid on an "offsite location", and observers objecting to the "assignment"; the author repeatedly records that no one has contacted him in person (IS-J01-20250812-R04, IS-J01-20250822-R02).
- **"Looking inward".** The author describes a new practice of seeking visual impressions of the speakers and records brief descriptions of them (IS-J01-20250818-R02, IS-J01-20250820-R03).

## Where to start
- IS-J01-20250804-ENTRY — explains the recording and transcription method used for the rest of Part 01.
- IS-J01-20250812-R04 — the author's long summary of what he sees as changes since spring.
- IS-J01-20250819-R03 — compares the intersection with the shelter and lists the day's takeaways.
- IS-J01-20250822-R04 — the author's own explanation of the intersection process before starting.
- IS-J01-20250822-R10 — the last recording of Part 01, closing the day's list count.

## The author's own entries
The administrative notes are the author's own reflections: he describes losing legal help and a job opportunity, his daily walking and limited money, his ear "checks", experiences he describes as connecting with other people while half-asleep, and repeated requests for contact, testimony and acknowledgement. He frames his role as reporting suggestions rather than asserting them, warns listeners not to act on statements from strangers, and states that he makes no accusation against organizations named in the statements (IS-J01-20250812-R05, IS-J01-20250822-R04).


===== RECAP: summaries/IS_SUM_J02_2025-08.md · ID: IS-SUM-J02-2025-08 =====

# Journal Part 02 — August 2025

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 7 entries and 38 recordings, 23–31 August 2025 (recording days 23–26, 30 and 31 August) · about 67,000 words · source: `journal/part-02/`, files dated 2025-08. These are the first files of the source document titled "Invisible Ships Pt. 02, Aug 23rd - Sept 28th 2025".

## What this month contains
Every day follows the same pattern. In the morning the author recaps the night before at a men's shelter in Denver. In the afternoon he records from the median of a busy intersection, where he stands with a cardboard sign. The entries say he repeats aloud what he hears, records it on his phone and transcribes it with an AI tool. At the intersection he switches between repeating each statement and staying silent, then summarising. Recordings run from a few seconds to over 40 minutes, and 23 August alone has 12.

Over the nine days the author describes the people he says he sees sending the statements in more detail, and more statements are recorded as threats. From 26 August he notes static and damaged audio at the intersection (IS-J02-20250826-R05, IS-J02-20250831-2-R02).

## Recurring statements
- **"Do not support this person."** Statements aimed at drivers repeat this line, and the recordings say drivers who respond are added to a euthanization list. On 23 August the author keeps a running count (IS-J02-20250823-R05, IS-J02-20250823-R06, IS-J02-20250823-R09).
- **Euthanization offered to the author.** Statements ask him to accept it. Others say it is the only thing they are ordered to offer him or call it an ethical standard. He refuses each time (IS-J02-20250824-R02, IS-J02-20250826-R02, IS-J02-20250825-R06).
- **Two groups, one workflow.** Statements say one group torments while another adds people to the list. When the author asks, a reply says both are one workflow (IS-J02-20250823-R11, IS-J02-20250824-R06).
- **One voice behind many.** The author says that when he listens past the filtered voices, eight to ten speakers turn out to be one woman taking both sides (IS-J02-20250824-R03, IS-J02-20250825-R01).
- **Sound heard by others.** Statements say people at the shelter and the intersection can hear the conversation with their ears. The author says one shelter resident asked him about it (IS-J02-20250824-R06, IS-J02-20250825-R04).
- **Implants and promised help.** Statements say the author has an implant or that implants are widespread. Others promise witness protection, and he records that no in-person contact follows (IS-J02-20250825-R04, IS-J02-20250825-R07, IS-J02-20250826-R08).
- **Threats and violent content.** The author logs a statement that the country will be bombed as the third threat of its kind (IS-J02-20250826-R06, IS-J02-20250826-R08). On 30–31 August he describes point-of-view accounts of killings and a reference to child abuse (IS-J02-20250830-R02, IS-J02-20250831-2-R01).

Other typical lines: "You look like monsters to normal people." (IS-J02-20250824-ENTRY); "I hate this assignment." (IS-J02-20250824-R07).

## Where to start
- IS-J02-20250823-ENTRY — the methodology and the daily scene.
- IS-J02-20250823-R04 — the author explains what happens at the intersection.
- IS-J02-20250823-R11 — end-of-day takeaways, including the two-groups question.
- IS-J02-20250824-R03 — the night recap describing the voice filters. IS-J02-20250824-R04 transcribes the same audio and differs in places.
- IS-J02-20250826-R07 — the author's themes from the intersection.

## The author's own entries
The entries hold the methodology, the scene, each day's list of recordings, and short captures typed on his phone with speakers marked by apparent gender (IS-J02-20250824-ENTRY, IS-J02-20250826-R03). Most of his commentary is in long spoken notes. In them he says he is not accusing any organization, asks for official statements and technology demonstrations, and says he will never accept euthanization. IS-J02-20250831-ENTRY says the earlier source document hit its character limit, so a new one was started.


===== RECAP: summaries/IS_SUM_J02_2025-09.md · ID: IS-SUM-J02-2025-09 =====

# Journal Part 02 — September 2025

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 14 entries and 56 recordings, 1–28 September 2025 (recording days 9–15, 19 and 24–28 September; 1 September has an entry only) · about 153,000 words · source: `journal/part-02/`, files dated 2025-09. These are the last files of source document "Invisible Ships Pt. 02".

## What this month contains
The daily pattern from August continues. In the morning the author recaps the night at a 300-person shelter in Denver. In the afternoon he stands at an intersection with a cardboard sign and alternates between repeating statements aloud, listening and summarising, and silence. The 1 September entry says recordings were "negatively impacted" and gives a written summary instead (IS-J02-20250901-ENTRY).

Three changes stand out. From 15 September, statements from a group claiming to speak for a foreign government appear, alongside claims about who operates the "systems" (IS-J02-20250915-R01, IS-J02-20250915-R02). From 26 September, the author reports experiencing other people's point of view while asleep (IS-J02-20250926-R01, IS-J02-20250927-R02). Small protests at the intersection on 13 and 27 September prompt statements about being sent there (IS-J02-20250913-R01, IS-J02-20250927-R05).

## Recurring statements
- **"Do not support this person (to the left of you)."** Aimed at drivers and often followed by "add them to the list." The author asks whether drivers actually responded (IS-J02-20250910-R01, IS-J02-20250912-R01, IS-J02-20250925-R04).
- **Accepting euthanization.** Statements ask why he has not accepted it. These include a statement asking if he would consider ending his own life and a whiteboard visual urging it. He refuses each time (IS-J02-20250915-R01, IS-J02-20250927-R01, IS-J02-20250927-R07).
- **Threats to family and to him.** Statements threaten his daughter and ex-wife if he keeps recording. Others claim harm to a longtime friend (IS-J02-20250909-R02, IS-J02-20250914-R01, IS-J02-20250925-R01).
- **"Word for word accuracy."** Speakers comment on whether his transcripts are accurate. The author analyses why this keeps coming up (IS-J02-20250909-R03, IS-J02-20250914-R04).
- **Who is speaking.** Statements name agencies, a contractor and foreign groups as operators, sometimes denying it later. The author says all of it must be substantiated (IS-J02-20250915-R04, IS-J02-20250928-R03, IS-J02-20250928-R04).
- **"Spirituality narrative" versus technology.** The author and respondents discuss whether the voices are presented as spirits to avoid seeking consent (IS-J02-20250927-R04).
- **Offers and lures.** Statements offer drugs, a ride in a van or money if he goes somewhere. The author calls these lures (IS-J02-20250919-R01, IS-J02-20250928-R07).
- **Attack claims.** Statements claim a terrorist attack since February, a nuclear threat and an attack overseas (IS-J02-20250919-R06, IS-J02-20250925-R05).

Typical lines: "This is absolutely the worst product launch." (IS-J02-20250909-R03); "Hold your ears shut and see if there's a difference." (IS-J02-20250927-R06).

## Where to start
- IS-J02-20250909-R04 — the author's spoken introduction to what happens at the intersection.
- IS-J02-20250915-R02 — the first long discussion with the group claiming to be a foreign government.
- IS-J02-20250924-R01 — his proposal for how a household could document and share what each member hears.
- IS-J02-20250926-R02 — his updated introduction, including the "contact tracing" comparison.
- IS-J02-20250927-R04 — a structured discussion of the "spirituality narrative."

## The author's own entries
The later entries switch to bulleted daily summaries (IS-J02-20250924-ENTRY, IS-J02-20250928-ENTRY). Several recordings end with written "Questions & Comments" asking for technical explanations and repeating that nothing is proof without testimony, official statements or technology demonstrations (IS-J02-20250915-R01, IS-J02-20250926-R01, IS-J02-20250927-R02). He also offers the document for donations. He proposes self-care steps, a daily intention ritual and household check-ins (IS-J02-20250910-R03, IS-J02-20250927-R03). Several recordings are described in their own headers as alcohol-fuelled releases of anger (IS-J02-20250915-R06, IS-J02-20250928-R09).

# Journal Part 03 — September 2025

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 1 entry and 3 recordings, 29 September 2025 · about 7,200 words · source: `journal/part-03/`, files dated 2025-09.

## What this month contains
Part 03 opens with a single day, Monday 29 September 2025. The entry (`IS-J03-20250929-ENTRY`) says the author is moving around downtown Denver by scooter and on foot, and restates his method: he repeats aloud the statements he receives while recording on a phone, and the audio is transcribed with an AI tool. The three recordings run from early afternoon to evening. Much of the day's material is the author speaking in his own voice rather than repeating statements: one recording is a long reflection on what relief and assistance could look like, and another reviews a pattern of statements he has collected over earlier months. Statements marked as coming from the distance appear mainly near the end of each recording.

## Recurring statements
- **Euthanization.** Statements and the author's notes describe people said to be coming to a street intersection to euthanize him and then deciding against it; the author lists methods the statements have suggested over time (`IS-J03-20250929-R03`).
- **A missing-person poster.** The author reads a public missing-person poster aloud; statements and his notes describe "telepathic visuals" suggesting the person was harmed (`IS-J03-20250929-R01`).
- **Threats to family.** The author's notes record a long-running pattern of statements that his daughter is dead or will be harmed (`IS-J03-20250929-R01`, `IS-J03-20250929-R03`).
- **Rescue and approach.** Statements say the author's description of how to approach him is useful and that something similar has "already started" elsewhere (`IS-J03-20250929-R02`, `IS-J03-20250929-R03`).
- **Pressure to accept euthanization.** A late statement urges the author to accept euthanization; he refuses on the recording (`IS-J03-20250929-R03`).
- **Change in tone.** The author estimates the communication has shifted from almost entirely hostile in February–March to roughly half defensive now (`IS-J03-20250929-R03`).

## Where to start
- `IS-J03-20250929-ENTRY` — the day's summary, scene and method.
- `IS-J03-20250929-R03` — the author's own overview of the intersection pattern and how he would want a rescuer to approach.
- `IS-J03-20250929-R02` — a 29-minute "envisioning" of comfort, media acknowledgement, legal help and a recovery retreat.
- `IS-J03-20250929-R01` — short; shows how a public poster becomes the subject of statements.

## The author's own entries
The entry is a three-bullet summary plus scene and method. The Questions & Comments under the first recording ask whether the communication exposes young people in the city to danger and record patterns of statements about euthanization and about his daughter. Across the recordings the author also describes his practical constraints (shelter, medication, staying in the metro area) and his conditions for trusting anyone who offers help: no questions on approach, written information, a video call, and an attorney.


===== RECAP: summaries/IS_SUM_J03_2025-10.md · ID: IS-SUM-J03-2025-10 =====

# Journal Part 03 — October 2025

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 11 entries and 36 recordings, 1–26 October 2025 · about 106,000 words · source: `journal/part-03/`, files dated 2025-10.

## What this month contains
Recording happens on nine days (1, 2, 11, 18, 19, 22, 24, 25 and 26 October); 12 and 16 October are short entries with no recordings, and there are no files for 3–10 October. The author moves around Denver and sleeps in shelters. Morning recordings recap the previous night: statements heard in the dormitory and detailed "point of view" visuals. Midday recordings come from a street intersection where he stands with a cardboard sign asking for donations, repeats aloud what he hears said to passing drivers, then switches to summarising. Several recordings are mostly in his own voice, including a self-care routine (`IS-J03-20251022-R02`, `IS-J03-20251022-R03`) and research notes on how assisted dying is regulated. The 16 October entry records that he has had no contact or help from any organisation he sent the transcripts to, can no longer use his transcription tool, and has posted a link to all the audio (`IS-J03-20251016-ENTRY`).

## Recurring statements
- **Pressure to accept euthanization.** The statements repeatedly ask him to accept euthanization, or to accept help from the people who would carry it out. The author argues that these phrasings mean the same thing (`IS-J03-20251001-R02`, `IS-J03-20251022-R07`).
- **The intersection and the "list".** Drivers are told not to support him, and statements say those who respond are added to a list. He estimates 30–50 people a week, and on 25 October he reports a quieter day (`IS-J03-20251001-R06`, `IS-J03-20251025-R06`). One statement says: "He's repeating people at the intersection, not communicating with the vehicles" (`IS-J03-20251022-R04`).
- **Technology and implants.** Statements say he has an implant, that a nanotechnology interface is widespread, or that no implant is needed. A new figure called "the hangman" speaks of punishment for treason (`IS-J03-20251001-R03`, `IS-J03-20251002-R01`, `IS-J03-20251022-R01`).
- **Agencies and drugs.** Statements name federal agencies, local police and companies, and offer him drugs or a job selling drugs. He calls these offers predatory (`IS-J03-20251002-R03`, `IS-J03-20251025-R02`).
- **Violent visuals.** He describes long night-time visuals of killings and torture at an "offsite location", along with shared smells. He says each time that they must be substantiated (`IS-J03-20251011-R01`, `IS-J03-20251018-R01`, `IS-J03-20251024-R02`).
- **Defence and doubt.** Some voices defend him and the shelter. One says "violence has discredited the method of communication" (`IS-J03-20251025-R01`). Another says "This person needs to be treated with dignity and respect" (`IS-J03-20251026-R01`).

## Where to start
- `IS-J03-20251001-R05` — his own introduction to the "ugly workflows" and to what he does at the intersection.
- `IS-J03-20251002-R02` — a long exchange on public awareness, consent and law enforcement.
- `IS-J03-20251022-R06` — his account of the "override" technique and other people at the intersection.
- `IS-J03-20251024-R03` — he asks whether the document could be manufactured and urges readers to check claims.

## The author's own entries
The entries are short bullet summaries; two contain only a placeholder ("…"). In the recordings he labels each line by who is speaking ("from the distance", "from the author"). He says repeatedly that the statements are not evidence on their own and that he accuses no organisation. He refuses euthanization each time it comes up. He reports ringing in the ears, nausea and a lung infection. He also sets out what safe first contact would look like: no questions in person, an envelope, a video call and an attorney.

# Journal Part 03 — November 2025

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 4 entries and 2 recordings, 5–8 November 2025 · about 22,000 words · source: `journal/part-03/`, files dated 2025-11.

## What this month contains
Part 03 ends with four consecutive days in early November; there are no files between 27 October and 4 November. The author is staying at a shelter in Denver. On 5 November his phone had died, so `IS-J03-20251105-ENTRY` is a written account of the intersection. `IS-J03-20251106-ENTRY` is a transcript he typed on a laptop at about 3:45 a.m. `IS-J03-20251108-ENTRY` (about 10,500 words) contains three transcribed recordings and two placeholder slots with no text. The only separate recording files are the two parts of the 7 November morning recap. He notes that the first part's audio was distorted and its transcript is unchecked.

## Recurring statements
- **A new sign and a new statement.** He holds a new sign naming a book about brain privacy. He records that, for three days in a row, drivers are told not to look the information up and that they will be punished if they do. He says this breaks a year-long pattern (`IS-J03-20251105-ENTRY`, `IS-J03-20251108-ENTRY`).
- **Voices reading the document.** Voices react as he types. They discuss voice filters and say "This document is accurate" (`IS-J03-20251106-ENTRY`).
- **Silencing.** He describes a sequence he has developed: overriding words, then sending sounds back, then "blotting out", which he says continues by itself. He says it brought silence to parts of the shelter. Statements report that people are losing costly "user accounts" (`IS-J03-20251107-R02`, `IS-J03-20251108-ENTRY`).
- **Implants and interfaces.** Statements say that most people now have a brain-computer interface, or that a "new ham radio" is in use by supply-chain workers (`IS-J03-20251107-R01`, `IS-J03-20251108-ENTRY`).
- **Euthanization and threats.** Statements about the euthanization list continue. One statement threatens him with execution. Others describe facilitators quitting their jobs or fearing prison (`IS-J03-20251107-R01`, `IS-J03-20251108-ENTRY`).

## Where to start
- `IS-J03-20251105-ENTRY` — a written description of the intersection and the new sign.
- `IS-J03-20251106-ENTRY` — a typed exchange that shows how he answers by "visualizing" words.
- `IS-J03-20251107-R02` — his own account of the silencing technique and its physical cost.
- `IS-J03-20251108-ENTRY` — the closing day: his stages of a "journey" and a day at the intersection.

## The author's own entries
In the entries he frames his experience as a series of stages: an "esoteric beginning", then "technological awareness", and now a stage of defence. He gives step-by-step instructions to others, and he warns them not to act on a voice's instructions. He repeats that claims "need to be substantiated", that his document accuses no organisation, and that he wants contact by email or phone rather than in person. He also records that people describe "how incredible silence feels" (`IS-J03-20251108-ENTRY`).


===== RECAP: summaries/IS_SUM_J04_2025-11.md · ID: IS-SUM-J04-2025-11 =====

# Journal Part 04 — November 2025

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 6 entries and 33 recordings, 11–29 November 2025 · about 83,000 words · source: `journal/part-04/`, files dated 2025-11. Part 04's source document (headers: "Invisible Ships Pt. 04") runs from 9 November 2025 onward.

## What this month contains
Recording took place on six days (11, 13, 14, 19, 21 and 29 November) in Denver. Most days open with a recording about the previous night at a large men's shelter where the author has a permanent bed. Long recordings follow from a six-lane boulevard median, where he holds a donation sign and repeats aloud, word for word, the statements he says are directed at passing vehicles.

Early in the month he changes his sign to recommend searching for a book about neurotechnology and mental privacy. After that, the recordings report a new vehicle statement alongside one that had stayed the same for a year. From 13 November they also capture unidentified groups that the author says are "overriding" the vehicle statements, changing their wording in real time. By 21 November these sessions make some recordings three times their usual length.

## Recurring statements
- **Vehicle statements at the intersection.** Statements tell drivers not to support the man with the sign. After the sign change they add a statement telling drivers not to search for that information (IS-J04-20251113-R04), sometimes with a threat of punishment. Drivers who respond are said to be added to a "euthanization list" (IS-J04-20251111-R03, IS-J04-20251129-R01).
- **Euthanization and "ugly workflows".** Repeated requests that the author accept euthanization. The statements describe a list, a "cleanup crew", financial suppression and a money motive. On 13 and 14 November, statements discuss shooting the author (IS-J04-20251113-R03, IS-J04-20251113-R04, IS-J04-20251114-R03).
- **Overrides by other groups.** Altered statements such as "This is not the spirit world. We're human beings." (IS-J04-20251113-R05). Speakers say they are documenting this, some with funding (IS-J04-20251114-R08, IS-J04-20251119-R04, IS-J04-20251121-R05).
- **Acknowledgment.** Calls for the country to "acknowledge what is happening". Statements agree with the author that the absence of acknowledgment is the attack's "linchpin" (IS-J04-20251119-R02, IS-J04-20251119-R05).
- **Body penetration and "digestive health research".** A group calling itself bowel-movement or digestive-health researchers, who say they want no part in euthanization (IS-J04-20251111-R01, IS-J04-20251119-R01).
- **Technology explanations.** Smoke detectors said to amplify the conversation, satellites, a wide-area surveillance system, an older satellite "used in seismic research", and implants (IS-J04-20251113-R01, IS-J04-20251113-R03, IS-J04-20251119-R05).
- **Recording interference.** The author describes a lost recording, outgoing calls on his phone and distorted audio (IS-J04-20251114-R02, IS-J04-20251114-R07).
- **Spiritual versus technological framing.** Self-described religious participants say neuroethics and consent are new ideas to them (IS-J04-20251114-R08, IS-J04-20251119-R05).

## Where to start
- IS-J04-20251113-R03: the author's introduction to the "ugly workflows" and the intersection.
- IS-J04-20251113-R05: the first capture of another group's overrides.
- IS-J04-20251113-R06: the author's proposal that households agree to discuss every statement openly.
- IS-J04-20251114-R01: questions and answers on the claimed motive.
- IS-J04-20251119-R02: the argument that the absence of acknowledgment is central.
- IS-J04-20251129-R01: a short, self-contained introduction for new readers.

## The author's own entries
The six entry files give a short daily summary, a fixed methodology note and a list of the day's recordings. In the recordings, the author repeats his critical disclaimer. He asks to be contacted by email, phone or video call rather than through the phenomenon, asks for donations and for help with the manuscript, and asks that the health-care system be informed. The 19 November entry (IS-J04-20251119-ENTRY) contains unfinished placeholder bullets.

# Journal Part 04 — December 2025

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 5 entries and 16 recordings, 2–9 December 2025 · about 49,000 words · source: `journal/part-04/`, files dated 2025-12.

## What this month contains
All of December falls in one week. On 2 and 4 December the author stands on the median of a busy Denver intersection with a sign recommending a book on artificial intelligence and brain activity, repeating aloud what he says is spoken to passing drivers (IS-J04-20251204-R01). On 5, 6 and 9 December the recordings become long spoken reviews of previous nights at a downtown men's shelter: four parts on 6 December (IS-J04-20251206-R01 to R04), five on 9 December (IS-J04-20251209-R01 to R05). Two recordings are separate transcriptions of one audio file (IS-J04-20251202-R02 and R03). On 9 December the author reports finding temporary data-entry work and a desk at a shared workspace.

## Recurring statements
- **Statements to drivers.** Drivers are said to be told not to support the person with the sign and, newly, not to search online for the book; those who respond are said to be added to a "euthanization list" (IS-J04-20251202-R02, IS-J04-20251204-R01).
- **Overrides.** On 4 December an unidentified group is described replacing the statements to drivers with a warning against communicating (IS-J04-20251204-R02).
- **Demands and attack claims.** Statements repeat that this is an attack on the country and list demands, including on trade and troop withdrawal (IS-J04-20251202-R03, IS-J04-20251206-R02).
- **Shelter events.** Statements at the shelter accuse another resident of child sexual abuse, share imagery the author describes as abuse of a child, and later say the resident accepted euthanization, while other statements say he is still there (IS-J04-20251209-R01, IS-J04-20251209-R02).
- **Experiments on the body.** Statements describe "cadaver research", mapping of the nervous system and digestive research, alongside back pain the author reports (IS-J04-20251209-R02, IS-J04-20251209-R03).
- **Threats.** Several recordings contain statements threatening violence or execution toward the author (IS-J04-20251202-R02, IS-J04-20251206-R03, IS-J04-20251204-R02).
- **A "union" of sources.** Statements name a sound-equipment company the author could not find, and several foreign countries as a "union" behind the communication (IS-J04-20251206-R02, IS-J04-20251209-R05).

## Where to start
- IS-J04-20251204-R01 — the clearest record of the intersection method.
- IS-J04-20251206-R01 — opens the four-part shelter review and describes "point of view" experiences during sleep.
- IS-J04-20251209-R01 — the multi-day review covering the shelter resident and the author's new work.
- IS-J04-20251206-R05 — short; the author's explanation of overlapping thought in brain-to-brain terms.

## The author's own entries
The entries are brief; the 9 December entry is a placeholder, and the 5 December entry notes a recording missing from his phone (IS-J04-20251205-ENTRY). In the recordings the author says the statements need verification, explains his annotation labels, asks for contact by phone or email, urges listeners not to harm themselves or others, and reflects on faith, research and martial arts (IS-J04-20251205-R01, IS-J04-20251209-R02).

# Journal Part 04 — January 2026

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 13 entries and 0 recordings, 2–26 January 2026 (two entries dated 14 January) · about 11,400 words · source: `journal/part-04/`, files dated 2026-01.

## What this month contains
January has no audio. Every file is a typed entry, mostly timestamped lists of quoted or paraphrased statements, written while the author stays as a guest in the Denver home of a friend who is also his employer. The 14 January short entry says he can focus only on a new job (IS-J04-20260114-ENTRY). By 26 January he has left the home, works from a shared workspace and is back at a shelter (IS-J04-20260126-ENTRY). The longest entries are 16 and 23 January (IS-J04-20260116-ENTRY, IS-J04-20260123-ENTRY).

## Recurring statements
- **Commentary on the document itself.** Many statements say the author is "typing every word", call the document accurate, repeat "Exclamation point", and refer to a "key command" or "keylogger" said to be interfering with his typing (IS-J04-20260106-ENTRY, IS-J04-20260123-ENTRY).
- **Attempts to enter the home.** Entries record statements asking the author, and on one night two children in the house, to open the door, and requests to "come outside" and raise a hand (IS-J04-20260102-ENTRY, IS-J04-20260104-ENTRY, IS-J04-20260125-ENTRY).
- **A detention facility.** Statements name a road address that the author looked up and found to be a detention center with an infirmary; later statements say experiments on prisoners began there (IS-J04-20260102-ENTRY, IS-J04-20260103-ENTRY).
- **Implants and the body.** Statements describe implants, silica in the body and "pain administration"; the author records visuals of workers in lab coats with a cadaver, alongside pain in matching parts of his body (IS-J04-20260103-ENTRY, IS-J04-20260126-ENTRY).
- **Radiation and sound.** Statements say the residence is being "radiated" or hit with EMF; the author reports an hour of high-pitched sound, nausea and ringing in his ears (IS-J04-20260104-ENTRY, IS-J04-20260123-ENTRY, IS-J04-20260125-ENTRY).
- **Speakers who withdraw.** Voices announce resignations, say they are leaving an "assignment" or call for federal oversight, while others continue to ask whether he accepts euthanization (IS-J04-20260116-ENTRY).
- **Threats.** Entries record statements about poisoning the author's food and other threats against him (IS-J04-20260116-ENTRY, IS-J04-20260125-ENTRY).

## Where to start
- IS-J04-20260116-ENTRY — the fullest entry, with a long Questions & Comments section.
- IS-J04-20260114-2-ENTRY — the author's account of earlier experiences in his sleep that statements linked to the entertainment industry, and his comparison with a new television drama.
- IS-J04-20260102-ENTRY — introduces the detention-facility theme.
- IS-J04-20260126-ENTRY — the move back to the shelter and the cadaver visuals.

## The author's own entries
In his Questions & Comments the author notes that the statements are external and need verification, asks where people can be tested for implants, asks whether statements are "pressured" to provoke reactions, questions a statement about a technology executive's satellites (IS-J04-20260119-ENTRY), and asks for funding for RF and EMF measurement. He says he has a year of negative drug tests, and he refuses on principle to go outside. One entry includes an AI-generated comparison table that the author pasted in (IS-J04-20260114-2-ENTRY).


===== RECAP: summaries/IS_SUM_J04_2026-02.md · ID: IS-SUM-J04-2026-02 =====

# Journal Part 04 — February 2026

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 3 entries and 0 recordings, 5, 10 and 13 February 2026 · about 4,300 words · source: `journal/part-04/`, files dated 2026-02.

## What this month contains
Three typed entries, no audio. The author is back in Denver's shelter system and writes from a coffee shop and from his dormitory bed. The 5 February entry explains that he lost access to the shared workspace, and his phone, after an encounter there with a woman he says he recognised from earlier visual experiences; he gives his own account of the incident and of the reason the workspace gave his employer (IS-J04-20260205-ENTRY). The 10 February entry is a live, timestamped capture of statements he says echo through a dormitory of about 150 people (IS-J04-20260210-ENTRY). The 13 February entry is organised by location (the intersection, the shelter) and then lists physical symptoms (IS-J04-20260213-ENTRY).

## Recurring statements
- **Physical descriptions.** The author lists more than a dozen people seen in visual experiences, including office workers with headsets, lab workers around a cadaver and a man described as carrying a sidearm (IS-J04-20260205-ENTRY).
- **Cadaver and nervous-system research.** Statements describe inserting a catheter into a cadaver "connected" to the author and building "a map of the nervous system" (IS-J04-20260205-ENTRY, IS-J04-20260213-ENTRY).
- **Accuracy of the transcripts.** As in January, statements remark that he is typing what they say, repeat "Exclamation point" and mention a "keylogger" (IS-J04-20260210-ENTRY).
- **A narcotics unit.** Paraphrased statements describe a local narcotics unit using fake profiles to support euthanization (IS-J04-20260210-ENTRY).
- **Intersection statements.** The author reports that drivers are still told not to support him or search for the book on his sign, and records a statement threatening an imminent attack on the country (IS-J04-20260213-ENTRY).
- **Pressure to go outside.** Shelter statements still ask him to come outside, raise his hand and accept euthanization, and ask whether he would "euthanize people" with the speakers (IS-J04-20260213-ENTRY).
- **Promised help that does not arrive.** Statements promise money or housing that, he notes, never arrives (IS-J04-20260210-ENTRY, IS-J04-20260213-ENTRY).

## Where to start
- IS-J04-20260213-ENTRY — the clearest overview: places, statements, symptoms and a dated summary of earlier terrorism-related statements.
- IS-J04-20260205-ENTRY — the workspace incident and the list of physical descriptions.
- IS-J04-20260210-ENTRY — an example of live capture in the dormitory.

## The author's own entries
The Questions & Comments sections ask why no speaker uses email or phone, and whether that behaviour fits criminal categories he names. They quote published definitions of domestic and international terrorism. They note that the record captures only a fraction of what he hears, and suggest that accusations in the statements may be attempts to probe the conversation. He also refers readers to the archive's Critical Disclaimer and quotes a passage from a reference document he wrote on brain-based detection techniques (IS-J04-20260210-ENTRY).


===== RECAP: summaries/IS_SUM_J04_2026-04.md · ID: IS-SUM-J04-2026-04 =====

# Journal Part 04 — April 2026

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 2 entries and 3 recordings, all dated 6 April 2026 · about 5,200 words · source: `journal/part-04/`, files dated 2026-04.

## What this month contains
After no entries in March, April has a single day. One entry is a short note in which the author says he believes he is in the most serious danger so far. He says he is isolating to protect friends, family and contracting clients, has no phone plan or working laptop, and expects no further updates without support (IS-J04-20260406-ENTRY). The second entry has no text of its own and only lists the day's three recordings (IS-J04-20260406-2-ENTRY; header `word_count: 0`). The recordings were made in the morning in downtown Denver, around a train station, between 9:13 and 10:13 (IS-J04-20260406-2-R01 to R03). In them the author uses a new title for the series, built around the phrase "decomposition tactics" delivered with neurotechnology.

## Recurring statements
- **A new label for the tactics.** Statements the author repeats describe the tactics as an "alternative" to a substance the author defines as blood harvested from people under terror. The author asks whether people who observe others through a brain-to-brain connection might experience their suffering (IS-J04-20260406-2-R01, IS-J04-20260406-2-R03).
- **Calls to shut the system down.** Statements, some attributed by the speakers to local police, call for "the system" in Denver to be turned off and for news-media acknowledgement (IS-J04-20260406-2-R01).
- **Ultimatums.** Statements repeat the earlier demand that the country "acknowledge what is happening", and a new paraphrased statement says attacks will continue regardless and frames them as retribution from the Middle East (IS-J04-20260406-2-R02, IS-J04-20260406-2-R03).
- **Street euthanizations.** The author describes visuals he received of people being held while given an injection, and asks whether they were real events or dramatizations (IS-J04-20260406-2-R02).
- **The perceptual set.** Statements thank the author for the idea that the situation lies outside people's "perceptual set", and tell listeners not to judge themselves for not noticing it (IS-J04-20260406-2-R02, IS-J04-20260406-2-R03).

## Where to start
- IS-J04-20260406-ENTRY — the author's own short statement of his situation.
- IS-J04-20260406-2-R01 — introduces the new framing and the reactions to it.
- IS-J04-20260406-2-R03 — the author's summary, his proposal for public RF/EMF scanning, and his argument for a "world with consent".

## The author's own entries
Most of the author's commentary is in the recordings. He says repeatedly that the statements are questions needing verification, and he urges listeners not to euthanize or observe anyone without consent. He proposes that any organisation could acknowledge the technology publicly and offer RF and EMF scanning to residents. He also asks whether hiding the technology is worth more than human life. R01 contains a spoken date error ("2020" for 2026).


===== RECAP: summaries/IS_SUM_J04_2026-05.md · ID: IS-SUM-J04-2026-05 =====

# Journal Part 04 — May 2026

*Recap written with AI assistance from the files listed below. It describes what the record contains; it is not part of the record and does not verify it. Read the source files before quoting.*

**Covers:** 1 entry and 0 recordings, 6 May 2026 · about 100 words · source: `journal/part-04/`, files dated 2026-05.

## What this month contains
A single short administrative entry and no audio (IS-J04-20260506-ENTRY). It is the last dated official entry in Part 04. The author writes that the threat of "decomposition tactics" and coerced or forced euthanasia still exists in Denver. He says the conversation he hears out loud is continuing, but he suggests that public outcry and public participation may be influencing a state- or federally sponsored change. Most of the rest of the entry is a practical notice: he has changed the handle of his payment account and updated its photo. He says that to date he has received no donation, no email with an explanation and no in-person contact.

Readers following the record past this point should go to `journal/discovery-notes/`. That folder holds the author's unofficial working notes from 9 April to 24 May 2026, which overlap this month (see IS-SUM-JDN).

## Recurring statements
No statements are quoted in this entry. It refers in general terms to the continuing threat of coerced euthanasia and the "decomposition tactics" framing used in April (IS-J04-20260506-ENTRY).

## Where to start
- IS-J04-20260506-ENTRY — the only file for the month.

## The author's own entries
The entry is entirely the author's own words: a brief status update, a note of possible change, and a change to how readers can send him money.

## Catalogue of every journal file

One line per file: ID · date · kind · words · themes. Themes are the archive's own tags (`meta/IS_META_categories.md`). Open the bundle for the Part to read a file.

## Headline findings, and what the archive does not establish


===== FILE: concepts/IS_CON_00_findings.md · ID: IS-CON-00-FINDINGS =====

# What this research found

*Independent research compiled from public records for informational purposes only. Not legal, medical, or investment advice. Every concept states its BASIS, its ORIGIN, its THEME and the readers it was written for. The basis tiers are ranked, and never blended inside a single concept: a reader who accepts only `documented` entries can still rely on every one of those and discard the rest without unpicking anything. `testimony` is a dated first-person report, verified by nobody, and says so on its own face wherever it appears. Causes are reported as attributed, never asserted. See `meta/IS_META_terms.md`.*

Five bodies of work on one page: a procurement record, a public-health record, a crime record, a master timeline, and the concepts drawn from all of them. Every figure resolves to a named source, every claim states what it rests on, and every one of them says what it does not answer.

Each figure below resolves to a named source inside the concept it links to.
None of them is a finding about any individual person, including the author.

## €90.5m

in fines against one facial-recognition company across four European regulators. Over the same period US Immigration and Customs Enforcement paid it $12.75m.

Read it in full: `IS_CON_fined-in-europe-hired-in-america.md`

## 190m

people had their records exposed in the 2024 Change Healthcare breach, at a clearing house no patient chose or had heard of.

Read it in full: `IS_CON_how-protected-is-your-medical-record.md`

## 420

schoolchildren were placed on a Florida sheriff's list of likely future criminals. Having been a victim of abuse was one of the things that could put a child on it.

Read it in full: `IS_CON_what-children-are-subject-to.md`

## 3,500

American newspapers have closed since 2005, and 213 counties now have no local news source at all. That is why an absence of coverage proves very little.

Read it in full: `IS_CON_why-isnt-this-in-the-news.md`

## $300

bought Reuters a human cervical spine. The trade is lawful in most states, and the donating family was never told.

Read it in full: `IS_CON_who-profits-from-a-body.md`

## 40%

rise in the United States suicide rate between 2000 and 2021, while the world's fell 27% on the same measure.

Read it in full: `IS_CON_us-rose-against-the-trend.md`

## 3

constitutional amendments a Florida sheriff's office admitted violating, in writing, to settle a case four residents refused to drop.

Read it in full: `IS_CON_what-children-are-subject-to.md`

## 1 in 3

healthy participants felt somebody standing behind them, touching them, when a robot delayed their own movement by half a second. Nobody was there.

Read it in full: `IS_CON_what-produces-the-feeling.md`

## What none of it establishes

- Nothing here establishes that any specific thing has been done to any specific person, including the author.
- No capability is documented that reads a person's perception, or that reaches them at a distance without their participation. Where a concept touches on that, it says so on its own page.
- An absence of evidence is recorded as an absence of evidence. It is never presented as proof that nothing happened, and never as proof that something did.
- Sources are cited for their own findings. Two sources sitting beside each other do not corroborate one another, and the section says so wherever they appear together.

Set out on its own in `IS_CON_00_not-established.md`, which is the file to
read first if you are here to check this archive rather than to use it.


===== FILE: concepts/IS_CON_00_not-established.md · ID: IS-CON-00-NOT-ESTABLISHED =====

# What this research does not establish

*Independent research compiled from public records for informational purposes only. Not legal, medical, or investment advice. Every concept states its BASIS, its ORIGIN, its THEME and the readers it was written for. The basis tiers are ranked, and never blended inside a single concept: a reader who accepts only `documented` entries can still rely on every one of those and discard the rest without unpicking anything. `testimony` is a dated first-person report, verified by nobody, and says so on its own face wherever it appears. Causes are reported as attributed, never asserted. See `meta/IS_META_terms.md`.*

These four limits stand over every concept, every chart and every table in
this archive. They are not a disclaimer bolted on at the end; they are the
conditions under which everything else here was written, and a reader is
entitled to hold the archive to them.

1. Nothing here establishes that any specific thing has been done to any specific person, including the author.

2. No capability is documented that reads a person's perception, or that reaches them at a distance without their participation. Where a concept touches on that, it says so on its own page.

3. An absence of evidence is recorded as an absence of evidence. It is never presented as proof that nothing happened, and never as proof that something did.

4. Sources are cited for their own findings. Two sources sitting beside each other do not corroborate one another, and the section says so wherever they appear together.

## Why this is a file of its own

An archive that states its own limits in a place a reader has to go looking
for has not really stated them. This file exists so the limits travel with
the download, and so they can be handed to an assistant on their own,
alongside any concept, without the concept's author choosing which parts
come along.

See also `meta/IS_META_terms.md` for the full legal notice, and
`IS_CON_00_findings.md` for the claims these limits apply to.

Transcripts and statements recorded in this archive are external communications and do NOT represent the author's beliefs, views, or intent. Nothing here accuses, blames, or alleges malfeasance by any named person, company, or government body, and all of this information requires independent verification.
